<debateSection name="#opening">

<heading>FRIDAY, 3RD MARCH, 1944.</heading>

<prayers>

<narrative>Mr. SPEAKER took the Chair at <recordedTime time="1944-03-03T11:05:00">11.5 a.m.</recordedTime></narrative>

</prayers>

<debateSection name="#irrigation_districts_adjustment_bill">

<heading>IRRIGATION DISTRICTS ADJUSTMENT BILL.</heading>

<p>Mr. RAUBENHEIMER presented a petition from M. C. L. Porter, W. H. Haig and D. J. Jager, riparion owners of the White River, District of Nelspruit, and W. S. Saunders, a farmer deriving water from the said river through the Manchester Noordwijk canal, in opposition to the Irrigation Districts Adjustment Bill.</p>

<speech by="#speaker">

<from>Mr. <person refersTo="hansard_za">SPEAKER</person>:</from>

<p>The Irrigation Districts Adjustment Bill is a hybrid Bill and under Standing Orders No. 264 (Public Business) and No. 45 (Private Bills) the petition should be signed by the petitioners themselves. Actually it has been signed by only the petitioners, the other three having authorised Mr. I. C. Silberbauer, by powers of attorney, to sign on their behalf. I am satisfied, however, that in this instance it was not possible for these three petitioners to sign the petition personally within the time required. Their powers of attorney were yesterday lodged with the Clerk of the House and as today is the last day on which a petition of this nature can be effective I have passed it for presentation.</p>

<p>Petition referred to the Select Committee on the Irrigation Districts Adjustment Bill.</p>

</speech>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#questions">

<heading>Questions.</heading>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Railways: Reduction of Tariffs.</heading>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours">

<num>I.</num>

<from>Dr. VAN NIEROP (for Mr. Klopper)</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Railways and Harbours:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether any reduction of tariffs in respect of traffic beyond the borders <span class="col_2411-2412" refersTo="page_1218"/>of the Union has been granted since 1st September, 1939, for (a) goods (b) tickets, (c) stock and (d) other traffic; if so, what reductions;</li>

<li>(2) what was the tonnage transported to date under such reductions; and</li>

<li>(3) what amounts were remitted by way of reduction of tariffs in respect of places outside the Union since 1st September, 1939.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours" to="#van_nierop">

<from>The MINISTER OF RAILWAYS AND HARBOURS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) The only rail tariff alteration effected since 1st September, 1939, in which local traffic railed to and from points within the Union and South-West Africa does not also share is the temporary tariff reduction of 33&#x2153; per cent. operative from 1st October, 1942, on goods traffic consigned to Elizabethville and more distant Belgian Congo destinations and to French Equatorial Africa.</li>

<li>(2) 15,707 tons up to 31st December, 1943.</li>

<li>(3) &#x00A3;17,103 up to 31st December, 1943.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Railways: Appeals Against Penalties.</heading>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours">

<num>III.</num>

<from>Dr. VAN NIEROP (for Mr. Klopper)</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Railways and Harbours:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many members of the staff lodged appeals against penalties imposed, (b) how many such appeals (i) succeeded and (ii) failed and (c) in how many appeals were the findings of the Board of Appeal not unanimous, during each of the years from 1941 to 1943; and</li>

<li>(2) (a) in how many appeals was the penalty imposed reduced or mitigated, (b) in how many cases of unanimous findings by the Board of Appeal did the General Manager not agree and (c) how many appeals came before the Railway Board, during each of the years from 1941 to 1943.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours" to="#van_nierop">

<from>The MINISTER OF RAILWAYS AND HARBOURS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) (a) &#x0026; (b) and (2) (a):<br/>

A portion of the information sought under these heads is no longer available and the extraction of the desired particulars in respect of the Years for which records are still held, affecting more than 70,000 members of the staff, would occupy a considerable period at a cost which could not be justified.</li>

</ol>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Defence Force: Treatment of Volunteer Wounded at Gazala.</heading>

<question by="#marwick" to="#minister_of_defence">

<num>VI.</num>

<from>Mr. MARWICK</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Defence:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether a volunteer No. 313352 I.L.H. was wounded in action at Gazala; if so,</li>

<li>(2) what injury did he suffer to his leg and to what extent was he disabled;</li>

<li>(3) what period did he spend in various hospitals;</li>

<li>(4) upon what date was he (a) discharged and (b) notified of his discharge;</li>

<li>(5) (a) what amount was paid to him in respect of (i) gratuity, (ii) payment in lieu of leave (for ex-North service) and (iii) one month&#x2019;s unpaid leave and (b) whether any such payments were not made to him; if so, why;</li>

<li>(6) whether he was approached by the military authorities whilst in hospital about his discharge;</li>

<li>(7) whether he signed any papers in connection with his discharge before being notified of the date of his discharge;</li>

<li>(8) whether he will be assisted to enable him to equip himself with the customary amount of civilian clothing and to provide for one dependant.; if not, why not; and</li>

<li>(9) what explanation is given for the treatment of this volunteer in connection with his discharge.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_finance" to="#marwick">

<from>The MINISTER OF FINANCE:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.</li>

<li>(2) A compound fracture of the right tibia sustained in action. The disability was assessed by the Commissioner of Pensions at 60&#x0025;.</li>

<li>(3) From 14th June, 1942 to date, except for various periods of sick leave granted to him between treatments. He is still in hospital.</li>

<li>(4)

<ol>

<li>(a) 31st December, 1943.</li>

<li>(b) 19th January, 1944.</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>(5)

<ol>

<li>(a)

<ol>

<li>(i) A cash allowance of &#x00A3;5.</li>

<li><span class="col_2415-2416" refersTo="page_1220"/>(ii) Cash payment in lieu of leave standing to the credit of volunteers is not made, the practice being to post-date discharges so as to include any such leave. In the case under notice, as the rank of the volunteer was that of a private, the post-dating of his discharge to a date embracing any leave to which he was entitled would not have been to his advantage as, whilst undergoing treatment, he was awarded a 100&#x0025; disability pension which exceeded the military pay and allowances of his rank. The pension became payable as from the 1st January, 1944.</li>

<li>(iii) The question of granting unpaid leave did not arise.</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>(b) Falls away.</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>(6) He was discharged in accordance with the recommendations of a medical board.</li>

<li>(7) No.</li>

<li>(8) Yes, he is being assisted to equip himself with civilian clothing. With regard to the question of an allowance for a dependant, the hon. member&#x2019;s attention is invited to section 9 of Act 44 of 1942.</li>

<li>(9) This volunteer&#x2019;s best interests were considered and the most beneficial treatment was accorded to him.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

<question by="#marwick" to="#minister_of_defence">

<from>&#x2020;Mr. MARWICK:</from>

<p>Arising out of the Minister&#x2019;s reply, can he tell the House whether this pensioner is better off in receipt of his present pension than he would have been had he continued to remain on the pay list?</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_defence" to="#marwick">

<from>The MINISTER OF DEFENCE:</from>

<p>I shall be glad if the hon. member will put his question on the Order Paper.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Senators and M.P.&#x2019;s in Military Service.</heading>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_defence">

<num>VII.</num>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many (a). Senators, (b) members and ex-members of the House of Assembly, (c) provincial councillors and ex-provincial councillors have resigned or been discharged from their military positions;</li>

<li>(2) (a) what are their names, and (b) when did their military service terminate;</li>

<li>(3) what military posts were or are being filled part time or full time by (a) Senators, (b) present and ex-members of the House of Assembly, and (c) present and ex-provincial councillors and by whom were or are such posts filled;</li>

<li>(4) what is the total amount drawn by each as salary and allowances to date; and</li>

<li>(5) whether any members of the House of Assembly fill military positions during the Parliamentary recess; if so, (a) who, and (b) what are their respective (i) ranks, (ii) duties, and (iii) salaries and allowances.</li>

</ol>

<oralStatements>

<heading><span class="col_2421-2422" refersTo="page_1223"/>Defence Force: Dispersal Camps.</heading>

<question by="#nel" to="#minister_of_defence">

<num>VIII.</num>

<from>Mr. NEL</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Defence:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) What is the number of dispersal camps in the Union for (a) European, and (b) non-European soldiers, and where are they situated;</li>

<li>(2) what is the number of (a) European and (b) non-European soldiers at present in such camps;</li>

<li>(3) what is the monthly cost of each camp; and</li>

<li>(4) whether any work is done by the men.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_finance" to="#nel">

<from>The MINISTER OF FINANCE:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) Seven, situated at Pretoria, Johannesburg, Pollsmoor (Cape Town), Durban, Port Elizabeth, East London and Tempe (Bloemfontein).</li>

<li>(b) Seven, situated at Johannesburg, Kimberley, Athlone (Cape Town), Port Elizabeth, Durban, Graaff-Reinet and Modder B (East Rand).</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>(2) (a) 1,169. (b) 2,998.</li>

<li>(3) I regret that this information is not available as the depots generally form a portion of larger camps and many services are common to both.</li>

<li>(4) Men who are fit perform normal military duties inside the camp, and on occasions are also employed on duties outside the camp. Men awaiting discharge on the ground of medical unfitness for military service are not required to perform any duties except such as are necessary for their own personal welfare. Where camp conditions permit they are encouraged to undertake voluntary work within the camp to keep themselves occupied, e.g., gardening, office work, guard duties, etc.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Sugar Cane Produced by Indians.</heading>

<question by="#nel" to="#minister_of_commerce_and_industries">

<num>IX.</num>

<from>Mr. NEL</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Commerce and Industries:</p>

<p>What percentage of the total production of sugar cane in Natal is produced by Indians.</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_social_welfare" to="#nel">

<from>The MINISTER OF SOCIAL WELFARE:</from>

<p>Approximately 8.5 per cent.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<p>Steps to Reduce Drunkenness.</p>

</answer>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_justice">

<num>XI.</num>

<from>Dr. VAN NIEROP</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Justice:</p>

<p>Whether any representations have been made to him for taking steps to bring about a reduction in drunkenness; and, if so (a) what representations, (b) by whom, (c) in what manner and (d) what steps have been taken in consequence.</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#van_nierop">

<from>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE:</from>

<p>Yes. (a) Various representations have been made (b) by diverse persons and bodies (c) both orally and in writing. These have in general been too vague to afford much guidance but the definite suggestion was made by the Interdepartmental Committee of Enquiry into Conditions on the Cape Flats that the conditions imposed by the Liquor Licensing Boards of Simonstown and Bellville should be extended to the districts of the Cape and Wynberg. I called a meeting of all the members of the four Boards concerned. At that meeting, which was presided over by the Secretary for Justice, there was a full discussion of many aspects of the problem, and the Simonstown and Bellville members confirmed the Cape Flats Committee&#x2019;s description of the beneficial effects of the so-called &#x201C;Simonstown&#x201D; condition. At their annual meetings the Cape and Wynberg Boards adopted a somewhat similar condition and I am glad to say that there was a definite reduction in the number of cases of drunkenness in both districts in January after that condition came into force.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Natives: Pass Fees.</heading>

<question by="#tothill" to="#minister_of_native_affairs">

<num>XII.</num>

<from>Mr. TOTHILL</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Native Affairs:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) What is the pass fee payable per month per native (a) on the Witwatersrand, (b) in Pretoria, (c) Potchefstroom, (d) Klerksdorp and (e) in the rural areas;</li>

<li>(2) what is the total collected in each of the above areas; and</li>

<li>(3) whether the Government will consider introducing legislation bringing about a uniform rate for pass fees; if so, when; if not, why not.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_native_affairs" to="#tothill">

<from>The MINISTER OF NATIVE AFFAIRS:</from>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p>(1)</p></td>

<td><p>(a)</p></td>

<td><p>Witwatersrand</p></td>

<td><p>2s.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>(b)</p></td>

<td><p>Pretoria</p></td>

<td><p>1s.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>(c)</p></td>

<td><p>Potchefstroom</p></td>

<td><p>1s.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>(d)</p></td>

<td><p>Klerksdorp</p></td>

<td><p>2s.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>(e)</p></td>

<td colspan="2"><p>The fees in 13 areas vary from 3d. to 1s. a month.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(2)</p></td>

<td><p>(a)</p></td>

<td><p>Witwatersrand</p></td>

<td><p>&#x00A3;48,184 monthly</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>(b)</p></td>

<td><p>Pretoria</p></td>

<td><p>&#x00A3;1,389 &#x201E;</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>(c)</p></td>

<td><p>Potchefstroom</p></td>

<td><p>&#x00A3;155 &#x201E;</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>(d)</p></td>

<td><p>Klerksdorp</p></td>

<td><p>&#x00A3;1,071 &#x201E;</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>(e)</p></td>

<td><p>Rural Areas</p></td>

<td><p>&#x00A3;2,815 &#x201E;</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<ol>

<li>(3) No. The circumstances of the different districts vary and the amount of the fees is fixed after careful consideration of all the facts.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Soil Conservation Instruction in Schools.</heading>

<question by="#ueckermann" to="#minister_of_education">

<num>XIII.</num>

<from>Major UECKERMANN</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Education:</p>

<p>Whether he will make representations to the provincial authorities (a) to include soil conservation as a subject of major importance in school curricula and (b) to encourage school principals to organise tours of areas where soil erosion is prevalent and to arrange visits by school children to experimental stations to study preventive measures.</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_lands" to="#ueckermann">

<from>The MINISTER OF LANDS:</from>

<p>(a) and (b) I shall be glad to transmit these requests to the provincial authorities.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Soil Conservation.</heading>

<question by="#ueckermann" to="#acting_minister_of_agriculture_and_forestry">

<num>XIV.</num>

<from>Maj. UECKERMANN</from>

<p>asked the ACTING MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND FORESTRY:</p>

<p>Whether he will consider introducing legislation to extend the scope of section fifteen of Act No. 13 of 1941 so as (a) to provide for a penalty to be imposed on any owner of land burning grass without a permit or the sanction of the officer in charge of soil conservation and (b) to compel all municipalities, village councils and land companies to adopt ways and means of adequately conserving land under their control, such conservation to be under the technical direction of the officer in charge of soil conservation.</p>

</question>

<answer by="#acting_minister_of_agriculture_and_forestry" to="#ueckermann">

<from>The ACTING MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND FORESTRY:</from>

<p>(a) and (b) The points raised by the Honourable Member will receive consideration in connection with further legislation which will be necessary to deal more effectively with the problem of soil conservation.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading><span class="col_2425-2426" refersTo="page_1225"/>Maize Crop.</heading>

<question by="#hemming" to="#acting_minister_of_agriculture_and_forestry">

<num>XV.</num>

<from>Mr. HEMMING</from>

<p>asked the Acting Minister of Agriculture and Forestry:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) What percentage of the normal maize crop is expected for the year 1944 in (a) the Union, and (b) the Transkeian Territories;</li>

<li>(2) whether there will be sufficient supplies available for human consumption;</li>

<li>(3) whether steps have been taken to make up any shortage of supply for human consumption in the Transkei;</li>

<li>(4) at what price per bag will maize be available for human consumption; and</li>

<li>(5) whether he will consider subsidising the purchase of mealies intended for human consumption so as to make supplies available at a price the human consumer can pay, on lines similar to those adopted for the stabilisation of the price of bread.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#acting_minister_of_agriculture_and_forestry" to="#hemming">

<from>The ACTING MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND FORESTRY:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1), (2) and (3) No estimate of the 1944-&#x2019;45 crop is yet available and it is too early in the season to gauge the extent of the damage caused by the excessive rains. It is expected that a more definite indication of crop prospects will be available towards the end of the month.</li>

<li>(4) No price has yet been decided upon for the coming crop.</li>

<li>(5) The question of the subsidisation of the consumption of maize is under consideration.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Municipal Public Health Department, Durban.</heading>

<question by="#marwick" to="#minister_of_public_health">

<num>XVI.</num>

<from>Mr. MARWICK</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Public Health:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether the report on the administration of the Municipal Public Health Department, Durban, by a committee presided over by Mr. Justice Broome has been submitted to him, if so,</li>

<li>(2) whether he is in a position to make a statement to this House as to what recommendations of the committee will be adopted by him; and</li>

<li>(3) what action has he taken in connection with the several matters of public concern which are dealt with in the report.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_public_health" to="#marwick">

<from>The MINISTER OF PUBLIC HEALTH:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.</li>

<li>(2) The findings and recommendations of the Committee of Enquiry have been accepted by the Department of Public Health.</li>

<li>(3) The Administrator has been approached with a view to the further action contemplated in Section 11 of the Public Health Act being taken.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Sheep Remedies.</heading>

<question by="#sauer" to="#acting_minister_of_agriculture_and_forestry">

<num>XVII.</num>

<from>Mr. SAUER</from>

<p>asked the Acting Minister of Agriculture and Forestry:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether tobacco extract (40 per cent. nicotine) and carbon tetrachloride used as remedies for sheep are unobtainable; and</li>

<li>(2) what steps does he intend taking to make these remedies available for farmers especially in areas where thread-worm and liver snails abound.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#acting_minister_of_agriculture_and_forestry" to="#sauer">

<from>The ACTING MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND FORESTRY:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) A small quantity of tobacco extract of the requisite strength is still obtainable in the Union, and an order sufficiently large for our veterinary requirements has been placed oversea. Carbon tetrachloride is at present not available in the Union, despite constant efforts made for a considerable time to import supplies.</li>

<li>(2) For wire-worm, the nodular worm remedy of the Department, or tobacco extract, may be used. Unfortunately no remedy is at present available for liver fluke, and farmers should meanwhile apply the preventive measures as explained in Departmental pamphlets which are obtainable at the Division of Veterinary Services, Onderstepoort.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister">

<num>XVIII.</num>

<from>Dr. VAN NIEROP</from>

<p>&#x2014;Reply standing over.</p>

</question>

<question by="#erasmus" to="#minister">

<num>XIX.</num>

<from>Mr. F. C. ERASMUS</from>

<p>&#x2014;Reply standing over.</p>

</question>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Railways: Manufacturing of Articles for Cavalcade.</heading>

<question by="#le_roux" to="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours">

<num>XX.</num>

<from>Mr. J. N. LE ROUX</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Railways and Harbours.</p>

<p>Whether employees in the Salt River workshops of the Administration are using railway tools and material for making <span class="col_2427-2428" refersTo="page_1226"/>toys and other articles such as garden benches, camp chairs, ash-trays and wheelbarrows for the Cavalcade; if so whether it is done with his knowledge and approval; and, if not, whether he will have it stopped.</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours" to="#le_roux">

<from>The MINISTER OF RAILWAYS AND HARBOURS:</from>

<p>Permission has been granted for articles of the types mentioned, to be made by workers who are interested in the Cavalcade, in their own time, mainly with their own tools, and with material paid for out of Cavalcade funds.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Railways: Collection for Prisoners-of-War.</heading>

<question by="#le_roux" to="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours">

<from>Mr. J. N. LE ROUX</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Railways and Harbours:</p>

<answer by="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours" to="#le_roux">

<from>The MINISTER OF RAILWAYS AND HARBOURS:</from>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Rate of Pay of Natives Employed by Lenz Munition Factory.</heading>

<question by="#marwick" to="#minister_of_commerce_and_industries">

<num>XXIV.</num>

<from>Mr. MARWICK</from>

<p>asked the Minister Of Commerce and Industries:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) What number of natives are employed by the Lenz Munition Factory, Johannesburg, and what is their rate of pay;</li>

<li>(2) whether pay is credited to natives who are temporarily ill through T.N.T. poisoning or other illness contracted in the factory; and if not,</li>

<li>(3) on what ground the pay of such natives is not credited.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_finance" to="#marwick">

<from>The MINISTER OF FINANCE:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) 2,440 natives were employed at Lenz Munition Factory as at 29th February, 1944. Rates of pay for ordinary labourers range from 1s. 10d. to 2s. 8d. per shift; for semi-skilled and boss boys from 2s. 10d. to 5s. 6d. per shift; all plus free food and housing accommodation and cost of living allowance.</li>

<li>(2) Yes.</li>

<li>(3) Falls away.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Farmers&#x2019; Applications for Rifles.</heading>

<question by="#strydom" to="#minister_of_justice">

<num>XXV.</num>

<from>Mr. J. G. STRYDOM</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Justice:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether it has been brought to his notice that farmers and others, when applying for permission to possess a rifle, are asked whether they are supporters of the Government and to what political party they belong; if so,</li>

<li>(2) whether he will give instructions for this practice to be stopped; and</li>

<li>(3) whether other or similar questions are put to applicants; if so, what questions.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#strydom">

<from>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) As far as I am aware, no such question is asked;</li>

<li>(2) Falls away;</li>

<li>(3) Every applicant is required to give reasons why he needs a rifle. The only questions asked are to verify those reasons. Rifles are issued only on approval of Chief Commandant, who usually obtains a report from the local magistrate. All applications are dealt with on their merits, irrespective of the political creed of the applicant.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister">

<num>XXVI.</num>

<from>Dr. VAN NIEROP</from>

<p>&#x2014;Reply standing over.</p>

</question>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Fencing and Improvement of Native Lands.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF NATIVE AFFAIRS replied to Question V by Mr. Nel standing over from 25th February:</p>

<question by="#nel" to="#minister_of_native_affairs">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<p>What was the amount spent from January, 1936, to December, 1943, on (a) the fencing of native lands and (b) the improvement of native land and agriculture.</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_native_affairs" to="#nel">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<p>The information is not readily available in the form in which it is asked and it would take a great deal of time and labour to extract it from the accounts. The hon. member&#x2019;s attention is, however, directed to the Reports of the Controller and Auditor-General, containing details of the expenditure of the South African Native Trust Fund, which have from time to time been placed on the table of this House.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Native Quarters Near Midway Settlement.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF FINANCE replied to Question XIII by Mr. Haywood standing over from 25th February:</p>

<question by="#haywood" to="#minister_of_finance">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether the settlers at Midway have made representations to his Department not to erect the native quarters of Flying School No. 27 directly opposite their homes; if so,</li>

<li>(2) on what date were such representations first made;</li>

<li>(3) on what date was the erection of the native quarters started;</li>

<li>(4) what is the distance from the native quarters to the nearest house on the Midway Settlement;</li>

<li>(5) whether the settlers have complained about the bad smell Of the sewage system;</li>

<li>(6) what is the distance from the sewage system to the nearest house;</li>

<li>(7) what is the average number of natives in the quarters;</li>

<li>(8) whether they have a playing field directly opposite the homes of the settlers; and</li>

<li>(9) whether native quarters of any other flying school are so near to the homes of Europeans; if so (a) at what place and (b) what is the distance?</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_finance" to="#haywood">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.</li>

<li>(2) 22nd October, 1941.</li>

<li>(3) Work was commenced in October, 1941. It was stopped in November, 1941, as a result of the representations received. The matter was examined with a view to preventing any possible nuisance from the N.M.C. housed in the camp and work was recommenced on a modified scheme in October, 1942.</li>

<li>(4) 100 yards.</li>

<li>(5) Yes. The matter was investigated and although it was found that there was no real cause for complaint, certain steps were taken to effect improvements.</li>

<li>(6) Approximately 400 yards.</li>

<li>(7) 380.</li>

<li>(8) No.</li>

<li>(9) I am having one other similar complaint investigated.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Broadcasting of Rugby Matches: Cape Western Union.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF POSTS AND TELEGRAPHS replied to Question XVII by Dr. Van Nierop standing over from 25th February:</p>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_posts_and_telegraphs">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether results or particulars of winter sporting events such as rugby matches will be broadcast during the coming season; and, if so,</li>

<li>(2) whether he will make representations and give this House the assurance that the matches of the Cape Western Union will be treated by the Broadcasting Corporation on an equal footing with those of other Unions; if not, why not?</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_posts_and_telegraphs" to="#van_nierop">

<heading><span class="col_2431-2432" refersTo="page_1228"/>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.</li>

<li>(2) The matter is under consideration by the Board of Governors of the South African Broadcasting Corporation. I am not in a position to give any information beyond this at present.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Railways: Promotion of Assistant General Manager (Commercial).</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF RAILWAYS AND HARBOURS replied to Question XXVI by Mr. J. G. Strydom standing over from 25th February:</p>

<question by="#strydom" to="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether the present Assistant General Manager (Commercial) when appointed to that post received promotion over the heads of other senior officials who were his seniors previously; if so,</li>

<li>(2) what are the names and grades of such officials;</li>

<li>(3) whether the appointment was made upon the unanimous recommendation of the Railway Service Commission; and, if not,</li>

<li>(4) (a) which members recommended him, (b) which member did not agree with the recommendation, and (c) who was recommended by the minority?</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours" to="#strydom">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.</li>

<li>(2) Mr. E. X. Brain, Assistant Chief Civil Engineer; Mr. W. Heckroodt, Chief Traffic Manager; Mr. M. M. Loubser, Chief Mechanical Engineer; Mr. J. S. de V. von Willich, Chief Civil Engineer; Mr. E. H. Wilson, Chairman, Economic Bureau.</li>

<li>(3) In terms of Officers&#x2019; Staff Regulation No. 4 (6) a majority recommendation of the Railway Service Commission is deemed to be a recommendation of the Commission.</li>

<li>(4) (a), (b) and (c). Fall away.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Discharged Volunteers: Pensions.</heading>

<question by="#marwick" to="#minister_of_finance">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Upon what dates, respectively, were volunteers C. Rhoda (No. 274878), S. Witbooi (No. 14857), J. Appollis (No. 20372), N. Seat (No. 11513), G. Roberts (No. 15632), C. Watson (No. 165696), S. Goodwin (No. 166607), A. Warrens (No. 13245), A. Theunissen (No. 111636), C. Simmrie (No. 286152), A. Adams (No. 13765), F. Benjamin (No. 301102), T. Hoosain (No. 11520), F. Cupido (No. 318882), F. Parish (No. 274240), H. Williams (No. 168018), J. Erasmus (No. 165652), P. Bailey (No. 17437), E. Shears (No. 13949), S. Hendricks (No. 15210), I. Samuels (No. 15322), C. Cloete (No. 12418), D. Abrahams (No. 13657), H. Coetzee (No. 14361), J. Hoffman (No. 12055), I. Andrews (No. 11194), W. Thomas (No. 13951), C. Davids (No. 11440), A. Olkers (No. 274237), P. Plaatjies (No. 285178), P. Seals (No. 25070), A. Julius (No. 25606), Springfield (No. 166929), P Francis (No. 25520), B. Alexander (No. 24322), C. Fink (No. 14378) and G. Dryden (No. 166813) discharged from full-time military service;</li>

<oralStatements>

<heading>National Road Transportation Board.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF RAILWAYS AND HARBOURS replied to Question IX by Mr. F. C. Erasmus standing over from 29th February:</p>

<question by="#erasmus" to="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) Who are the members of the National Road Transportation Board,</li>

<li>(b) what are their salaries and allowances, and</li>

<li>(c) who appointed them;</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>(2) what are the functions of the Board:</li>

<li>(3) from what sources does it derive its funds;</li>

<li>(4) what approximate amount does it annually appropriate for advertisements; and</li>

<li><span class="col_2433-2434" refersTo="page_1229"/>(5) in what (a) ordinary and (b) motor magazines has the Board since its inception placed advertisements and what was the amount paid for such advertisements in respect of each of the magazines?</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours" to="#erasmus">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) (a) and (b):</li>

</ol>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p>Name.</p></td>

<td><p>Salary.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>A. C. Fenwick (Chairman)</p></td>

<td><p>&#x00A3;3 3s. per diem.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>G. W. Reid (Vice Chairman)</p></td>

<td><p>Seconded Railway Official&#x2014;receives no payment for services on Council but draws temporary allowance of &#x00A3;50 per annum in respect of higher responsibility.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Dr. H. Reitz (Member).</p></td>

<td><p>Receives no payment in respect of services on the Council. Dr. Reitz is also Chairman of the Central Road Transportation Board.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>R. E. FitzGibbon (Member)</p></td>

<td><p>&#x00A3;1,000 per annum.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>J. A. Bromley (Member)</p></td>

<td><p>&#x00A3;1,000 per annum plus a temporary allowance of &#x00A3;564 p.a. until the 30th June, 1944, to compensate for loss of salary from Durban City Council owing to release before superannuation age.</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<p>All these gentlemen receive travelling expenses at the rate of 17s. 6d. per diem, which has been temporarily enhanced by 25 per cent.</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) (c) They were appointed by the Minister of Railways and Harbours and the Minister of Commerce and Industries.</li>

<li>(2) The functions of the National Road Transportation Council comprise the conservation of road transport generally and more particularly the exercising of the powers conferred by Regulations 2 and 7 of the regulations promulgated under Proclamation No 59 of 1943.</li>

<li>(3) The Council has no funds at its disposal. Its expenses are at present defrayed from the Railways and Harbours Fund. As a result of the establishment of the Department of Transport this expenditure will, as from the 1st April, 1944, be defrayed from the Consolidated Revenue Fund.</li>

<li>(4) There is no annual appropriation for advertisements. During the current financial year an amount of &#x00A3;13,000 was provided for publicity and propaganda in connection with the &#x201C;Save Transport&#x201D; campaign.</li>

</ol>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p>(5)</p></td>

<td><p>(a)</p></td>
